#2833. Reggie Wayne jokes he should become ‘player-coach’ after Colts’ trade deadline sell-off

Reggie Wayne has not suited up in an NFL game since Jan. 18, 2015, when the Colts lost to the Patriots in the AFC Championship game.

He might need to get himself ready — just in case — after the Colts traded away two wide receivers prior to the NFL trade deadline on Tuesday.

The Colts' wide receiver coach joked about it on social media amid a busy day for the 2-7 Colts, which saw the franchise trade wideouts Keenan Allen and Josh Downs, thinning Indianapolis even further at the position.

"Welp… Might be time to dust off the old Reeboks…. Has the player-Coach thing ever happened before?" Wayne, a six-time Pro Bowler during his 14-year career in the NFL, wrote on X.

Wide receivers coach Reggie Wayne of the Indianapolis Colts looks on during the Indianapolis Colts Training Camp at Grand Park Sports Campus on Aug. 4, 2026, in Westfield, Indiana. Getty Images

Player-coaches have occurred in the past, but the last one was Dan Reeves in the early 1970s, when he was part of the Cowboys organization. However, it is no longer something feasible because it was banned in the current collective bargaining agreement.

Wayne put up 14,345 receiving yards and had 82 touchdowns during his career, which also included being named a three-time All-Pro and having eight seasons in which he had 1,000 receiving yards.

The Colts are down to five receivers on their active roster currently and have two others — Alec Pierce and D.J. Montgomery — on injured reserve.

Indy traded Downs to the Chiefs in exchange for 2027 fourth- and fifth-round picks. They sent Allen to the 49ers for a sixth-round pick in the 2027 draft.

The Colts are now down to Deion Burks, Ashton Dulin, Anthony Gould, Laquon Treadwell and Nick Westbrook-Ikhine, who was signed to the 53-man roster from the practice squad on Tuesday.

Indianapolis has not won a game since Oct. 4, when it defeated the Commanders, and has since dropped five straight games heading into the Week 10 matchup against the Dolphins.

#2832. Fed's Goolsbee calls inflation report 'borderline disturbing' as central bank weighs more hikes

Chicago Federal Reserve President Austan Goolsbee called the latest inflation data "borderline disturbing."

"It's another distressing report — borderline disturbing," Goolsbee said in an interview with PBS Tuesday.

"We have got a real inflation issue on our hands that has been developing," Goolsbee said, according to a transcript of his remarks. "We're going to have to deal with that."

The consumer price index rose 0.4% on a seasonally adjusted basis in October, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics' Tuesday release. That was higher than economists had predicted.

Goolsbee said he had not seen drivers of prices like tariffs fade over time like he hoped. He also said continuously high inflation on services concerned him.

And the Chicago Fed chief said lofty prices are a top issue for many people he meets.

"It's on everyone's mind," Goolsbee said. "It's on business mind. It's on nonprofits' minds. It's on ordinary consumers' mind."

The inflation report landed at a critical juncture for the Federal Reserve, as it considers its next interest-rate move — and the moves it may have to make beyond that if the numbers continue to come in hot.

Goolsbee's comments came on the heels of remarks from other Fed officials Tuesday who said the rate-setting Federal Open Market Committee may need to act more decisively to rein in inflation.

When it raises or lowers rates, the Fed typically moves them by a quarter-percentage point. But Fed Governor Christopher Waller said in London early Tuesday that a hot inflation report could push him toward advocating for an outsized half-point increase in December, and specifically said such a hike should be on the table if core CPI was "around 0.3." Hours later, that's exactly what happened.

Dallas Fed President Lorie Logan, speaking Tuesday after the inflation report, said she sees a need for two additional quarter-point increases, and said "it may be possible to move more rapidly this winter than the FOMC has moved so far this fall."

The rate-setting committee raised rates by a quarter point in September before voting to hold them steady in October.

After years of higher-than-preferred price growth, Goolsbee said the possibility of consumers and businesses beginning to expect hot inflation is a "major concern." If that does happen, he said it can make the process of cooling inflation "much more painful."

But Goolsbee didn't specify whether he thought the Fed should hike rates at its December policy gathering as a result of these concerns."I'm not a fan of being very specific and tying our hands at the next meeting or meetings in the future," Goolsbee said.

Waller, Logan and Paulson are voting members of the FOMC this year, which means they will cast votes on whether to raise rates at the committee's final meeting of the year in December. Goolsbee will be a voting member next year.

In the wake of the inflation report and the remarks from the various Fed officials, Fed funds futures are now unanimously expecting a rate hike at the December meeting, according to CME's FedWatch tool.

Now the question is about whether the central bank will raise rates by a quarter or half percentage point. The Fed funds futures currently put the odds of a half-point hike at 15%.

#2831. NFL trade deadline winners and losers: Vikings go full dadcore with Kirk Cousins in Super Bowl quest

After what happened on Monday night, the Minnesota Vikings had to do this. In a dispiriting loss to the Buffalo Bills, the Vikings saw their tremendous defense go to waste, as Carson Wentz completed eight of 25 passes for 81 yards, no touchdowns, two interceptions, and a passer rating of 8.9. It was one of the worst quarterback performances in recent history, and it continued a run of recent QB futility for the Vikings, from JJ McCarthy to Kyler Murray to Wentz. Vikings defensive coordinator Brian Flores is out-scheming every other coach in the league right now with his laboratory of blitzes and coverages. But it's starting to matter less and less, because opposing offenses know that you can wait that defense out when there's no juice on the other side of the ball. The Vikings rank first in scoring defense (116 points allowed), but they're 27th in points scored (146).

This is not sustainable, which is why Minnesota welcomed ur-suburban dad Kirk Cousins back to the team he led from 2018-2023 by sending a 2027 third-round pick to the Las Vegas Raiders. The 38-year-old is in his 15th NFL season, and he's not quite the player he used to be, but he's a massive improvement over everyone else in the Vikings building at his position. This season, Cousins has completed 202 of 315 passes for 2,340 yards, 20 touchdowns, eight interceptions, and a passer rating of 97.1. The rest of the Vikings QBs this season have combined for 137 of 225 passes for 1,286 yards, eight touchdowns, five interceptions, and a passer rating of 82.0.

Cousins enters an NFC North that may be the most brutally competitive division in NFL history, but if he plays like he has this season so far, the Vikings have more than a puncher's chance.

Do the Vikings like that? We suspect that they do. And in addition to his play on the field, he should bring a maturity and calm to the quarterback position in Minnesota, something that McCarthy and Murray's critics have said they have lacked at points during their careers.

Related: Joe Burrow is reviving the Bengals and pulling away in the race for MVP

As for the Raiders, they essentially pick up a free third-rounder after signing Cousins to a one-year, $11.3m fully guaranteed contract, and selecting Indiana quarterback Fernando Mendoza with the first overall pick in the 2026 draft. The plan all along was for Cousins to start until Mendoza was ready for the rigors of the NFL. We're about to see if he is.

It usually takes a minute for a traded player, no matter how good he may be, to make a considerable difference with his new team. That player has to deal with a new city, teammates and schemes– it's not an easy adjustment. But when the Chicago Bears traded 2027 and 2028 first-round draft picks and a 2027 fourth-rounder to the Tennessee Titans for defensive tackle Jeffery Simmons and a 2028 fifth-round draft pick … well, it cost a lot, but it made Chicago's defense better – immediately. Per SumerSports, the Bears have gone from 6.39 to 4.45 yards per play allowed with Simmons on the field, and he's made other Chicago defenders better, from edge rusher (and former Mississippi State teammate) Montez Sweat to linebacker Devin Bush.

In Chicago's 27-24 Sunday night win over the Tampa Bay Buccaneers, it was that defense which took over, as the usually potent Bears offense had its issues against the Bucs' top-tier defense. Caleb Williams threw four interceptions, and Chicago won a game in which they committed five or more turnovers for the first time since they did so twice in the 2006 season – including a 24-23 win over the Arizona Cardinals that led to the greatest postgame press conference ever, courtesy of Cardinals head coach Dennis Green.

With the offense playing at its usual pace and the defense at an entirely new level, the Simmons trade is a prime example of what a big-time trade should do – turn a good team into a great one.

The Seattle Seahawks needed to revive a run game that has been below average since the team let last season's Super Bowl MVP, Kenneth Walker III, walk to the Kansas City Chiefs. They believed they can solve their rushing problem by sending a 2027 third-round draft pick and a 2028 fifth-round draft pick to the Indianapolis Colts for offensive guard Quenton Nelson, the eight-time Pro Bowler who will eventually find his way to the Hall of Fame.

Nelson still has plenty to offer at the age of 30. But these deals don't always work out the way teams hope. Seattle's first game with Nelson was a 34-17 loss to the Bears, who came into Lumen Field with Simmons in tow, and wrecked any offensive plans the Seahawks made. Nelson is a left guard, which meant that the Seahawks moved 2025 first-round pick Grey Zabel to the right side, where he hadn't played since 2023, when he was at North Dakota State.

Moving from one side to the other is never as easy for offensive linemen as people may think, and the Bears wisely lined Simmons up more on Zabel's side than Nelson's – 88% to 12%, per Pro Football Focus. This allowed Simmons and his energized cohorts to pressure Sam Darnold ceaselessly, and the quarterback is now out with a quadriceps injury and a cracked rib.

Backup Drew Lock started Seattle's win over the Arizona Cardinals last week, and Zabel moved back to the left side, because Nelson is now recovering from a knee procedure, and is week-to-week.

Why is this a problem? Because Nelson won't have time to acclimate to his new line, and Zabel won't have time to acclimate to his new right side. Plus, Nelson is in the final year of the four-year, $80m contract extension he signed in 2022. If Nelson misses significant time, and the 5-4 Seahawks can't find their way into the playoffs, whatever positive effects Nelson's acquisition might have provided will be meaningless in the long term.

It has become more than apparent that the Jacksonville Jaguars are all-in this season. Which makes sense. The 6-2 Jags lead the NFL in point differential (+108) and turnover differential (+9), and after their 33-10 win over the Baltimore Ravens last Thursday, they rank first overall in DVOA for the first time in the history of the franchise – which goes back to 1996.

Which is why general manager James Gladstone made the call to trade a 2027 second-round pick and a 2028 fifth-round pick to the Atlanta Falcons for safety Jessie Bates III. Jacksonville have been assembling their safety rotation with spit and baling wire of late and have a lot of injuries to deal with, and Bates is one of the NFL's better and more versatile players at his position(s). He's an expert defender everywhere from the box to the slot to the deep third.

The Jags also traded a sixth-round pick for New Orleans Saints interior defensive lineman Davon Godchaux. This also works because the 6ft 3in, 330lbs Godchaux is a good rotational run defender, and the Jags recently lost DaVon Hamilton, the heartbeat of their run defense, to a season-ending injury.

But as it turns out, those moves were just the opening acts. Gladstone then made a massive deal with the Philadelphia Eagles, trading a 2027 first-round pick and a 2029 second-round pick for defensive lineman Jordan Davis.

And this is where Gladstone and the Jaguars may be a bit over their skis.

Selected with the 13th overall pick in the 2022 draft out of Georgia, Davis was a highly-regarded interior disruptor based on his college work, but that promise never quite developed in the ways in which the Eagles hoped. The 6ft 6in, 336lbs Davis has all the athleticism and power you'd want, but the results have been middling to disappointing. Davis has never amassed more than five sacks and 28 pressures in a season. Those totals both came in 2025, after which the Eagles signed him to a three-year, $78m contract extension.

Davis has become an above-average run defender, but when you're giving up future first- and second-round picks for a player, you want more than that, right? Especially when you consider what the Bears paid for Simmons, and what they've gotten out of that deal already. Simmons is a truly transformative player; Davis at his level best is more of a well-turned cog in a machine.

Maybe Jaguars defensive coordinator Anthony Campanile can extract more from Davis than his Eagles counterpart, Vic Fangio, ever did. But given Fangio's overall expertise (more than half the defenses in the NFL apply at least some of Fangio's principles), it's a steep bet at that price.

– The San Francisco 49ers poked around the Colts' fire sale and plucked out DeForest Buckner for a fourth-round pick, and receiver Keenan Allen for a sixth-round pick. These weren't what you'd call franchise-transforming moves, but Kyle Shanahan's offense can really use some receiver help with all the injuries they've seen this season, and Buckner can add to a defensive line that has been similarly beat up. As always, it is impossible to write more than two sentences about the 49ers without mentioning injuries, and that dastardly substation curse.

– Speaking of Colts receivers, the Chiefs acquired Josh Downs and a seventh-round draft pick for fourth-round and fifth-round picks. Patrick Mahomes's team have been in receiver purgatory for a long time, and while Downs won't blow you away with his production (33 catches on 57 targets this season for 354 yards and a touchdown), he can be one of those sneaky-good guys with an understanding of the nuances of his position. The Chiefs' leading non-Travis Kelce receiver this season is Xavier Worthy, who has 278 receiving yards and a touchdown. Maybe Downs fills in the gaps. He certainly seems happy about his new home.

– The Bills made one of the day's smartest moves when they sent a second-round pick to the Titans for safety Amani Hooker and a fifth-round pick. Buffalo's safety situation has been less than optimal in 2026 – between CJ Gardner-Johnson (who was released after poor play and a spitting suspension), Cole Bishop, and Damar Hamlin, defensive coordinator Jim Leonhard has had issues. Hooker comes in with far more of a pedigree – this season, he's tied with Xavier McKinney of the Green Bay Packers for the NFL's most interceptions with five, and the veteran will help to knit the entire pass defense together.

#2830. Back in Philadelphia with Celtics, Paul George looks forward to ‘a regular game'

Back in Philadelphia with Celtics, Paul George looks forward to 'a regular game' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Paul George's presence back in town was one of the many swirling morning storylines at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

For good reason, former Celtics teammates Jaylen Brown and Jayson Tatum will draw immense attention Tuesday night. After all, Brown has said there "might be a little lamb chops with some gravy" between the two, if not a full-blown beef.

But George's return to Philadelphia is more than a tiny subplot. Following two seasons in Philadelphia, he was the centerpiece of the Sixers' summer trade package to acquire Brown.

George told reporters before the Celtics' shootaround that it feels like "a regular game."

"Had I been here eight, nine, 10 years, different story," he said. "I was here two years. Obviously, we didn't have the best of luck or had a great, I think, partnership in terms of my ability to stay healthy and on the floor. So that kind of played into a lot of it.

"But obviously, it's a tremendous fan base, a great sports town to play in. So that was appreciated. There's no ill will for me. It'll be fun to just compete. And outside of that, it's a normal game."

The 36-year-old George indeed had trouble staying available for sustained stretches as a Sixer. He dealt with a variety of injuries and served a 25-game suspension last season for violating the NBA's anti-drug policy.

George did play well on both ends of the floor for much of the 2026 playoffs. He helped the Sixers come back to win a seven-game first-round series against the Celtics in historic fashion. The nine-time All-Star also brought a veteran steadiness to the Sixers' locker room and was glad to mentor younger players like VJ Edgecombe.

"Just his routine … even when he got hot, he didn't try to force it," Jabari Walker said Tuesday morning. "His game was smooth and I think that's how he is as a person, just not forcing anything and having a calm energy."

George has certainly enjoyed his early days as a Celtic.

Through nine games, he's averaged 20.4 points, 5.2 rebounds, 4.1 assists and 2.3 steals while shooting 42.5 percent from three-point range. Boston will enter Tuesday night's matchup second in the Eastern Conference standings at 8-2, the Sixers first at 9-2.

"I've been kind of blown away," George said. "I think it exceeded my expectations since being in Boston. You never know how much culture is involved and how much culture changes your outlook on things and the effect it has mentally, physically, just being in an environment … and the culture that Boston presents.

"So I've been thrilled. I've been playing some of my best basketball of late, and just been enjoying grinding it out with these guys out here. It's a fun style of play and I can't talk (enough) about just the thrill, the happiness and the excitement and joy that it has brought being here."

He was asked what sort of reception he expects from Sixers fans.

"Not sure," George said. "In some ways, I helped land them a future piece for them. So with that, not sure how the reception would be."

#2829. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2828. Recapping the NFL trade deadline for the Broncos

The Denver Broncos ended up adding one new player ahead of the NFL's trade deadline on Tuesday, acquiring linebacker Daiyan Henley through a trade with the Los Angeles Chargers.

The Broncos also explored options at tight end, safety and running back, with Jonathan Taylor, Tony Pollard and Trey McBride among the big-name players Denver checked in on.

The Broncos also had conversations with other clubs about in-house running back J.K. Dobbins and tight end Evan Engram, but both of those players ended up remaining in Denver.

In addition to adding Henley, the Broncos could get several reinforcements back from injuries after their Week 10 bye.

There is hope that wide receiver Jaylen Waddle (concussion), safety Brandon Jones (hamstring), cornerback Riley Moss (concussion), safety Talanoa Hufanga (ankle) and cornerback Kris Abrams-Draine (ankle) could return in time for a Week 11 showdown with the Las Vegas Raiders.

Additionally, defensive lineman Eyioma Uwazurike (shoulder) does not have a season-ending injury, so he is expected to return from injured reserve later this season. Denver running backs Tyler Badie (thumb) and RJ Harvey (knee) are eligible to return from IR in Weeks 12 and 13, respectively. And outside linebacker Jonah Elliss (ankle) is also eligible to return in Week 12.

So, more reinforcements are on the way for the 7-2 Broncos following their mid-season break.

#2827. The Kirk Cousins contract points to a revised deal or termination after 2026

The last time the Vikings brought back a key former player for a third-round pick, the reunion didn’t last long. This time, it will last longer — but possibly not beyond the current season.

In 2010, the Vikings sent a third-round pick to the Patriots for receiver Randy Moss, whom the Vikings had traded to the Raiders in 2005. Former coach Brad Childress, calling Moss a “programmatic non-fit,” released Moss after four games.

Now, quarterback Kirk Cousins returns for a third-round pick, and a contract that covers nine regular-season games. His deal with the Raiders was structured to maximize the amount the Falcons owed Cousins for 2026 ($8.7 million, on top of the $90 million he made in 2024 and 2025) with another $10 million paid by the Raiders to Cousins in 2027, in the form of a guaranteed roster bonus not subject to offset.

The contract also includes a 2028 base salary of $78.655 million that becomes fully guaranteed on the fifth day of the 2027 league year. Coupled with a $1.345 million base salary in 2027 that becomes fully guaranteed that same day, the Vikings would be on the hook for $80 million over two years if he’s not released by then.

Without a revised deal, the current contract surely would be torn up by March. The question is whether the Vikings and Cousins would be able to come up with a new contract to replace it.

Much of that depends on how the rest of the year goes. Still, Cousins has the ability to force his way to free agency, for the fourth time in his career, by simply declining to re-do the existing deal.

However it plays out, Cousins can be counted on to make a good business decision. He has $333 million in career earnings, which has him at fourth on the all-time list.

On-field success is a different story. He has made it to the postseason three times in his career, with one playoff win.

If, as expected, the return of Cousins provides a B-12 shot to the Minnesota franchise, he has a realistic chance of getting back to the playoffs. And of winning a second postseason game.

And maybe a third. Hell, maybe even a fourth.

#2826. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

#2825. Aaron Rodgers is one win away from tying Drew Brees for fourth-most of all time

If the Steelers beat the Bengals on Monday night, Aaron Rodgers will move into a tie for fourth time on the NFL's all-time wins list.

Rodgers' teams have won 180 games he has started, counting the playoffs. His next win will give him No. 181, the same number Drew Brees retired with, and the fourth most of all time.

The only quarterbacks in NFL history who have won more starts are Tom Brady with a preposterous 286 wins, Peyton Manning with 200 and Brett Favre with 199. Rodgers has said this will be his last NFL season, so he won't catch Favre for third place.

Rodgers won 158 games as starting quarterback for the Packers, six with the Jets and has now won 16 with the Steelers.

Green Bay defensive end Micah Parsons (1) sacks Detroit Lions quarterback Jared Goff (16) on Sunday, September 7, 2025, at Lambeau Field in Green Bay, Wis. The Packers won the game, 27-13.Tork Mason/USA TODAY NETWORK-Wisconsin

#2821. ‘Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics

'Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown was asked Sunday whether he and Jayson Tatum had spoken since the former teammates addressed their relationship during the preseason.

Does he plan to approach Tatum before Tuesday night's game at Xfinity Mobile Arena?

"I mean, he's on his side, I'm on my side," Brown said. "We're playing basketball. Both of us are trying to win. So it's nothing to talk about. Let's check the ball up."

After months of public comments, documentaries and questions about how their partnership ended, the basketball will finally take over.

Brown will face Boston for the first time since the Celtics traded him to Philadelphia for Paul George and four future draft assets in July. He spent his first 10 NBA seasons in Boston, played nine alongside Tatum and helped the Celtics win the 2024 championship, earning Finals MVP honors.

Tuesday's 8 p.m. game on NBC will also bring together the top two teams in the Eastern Conference. The Sixers are 9-2. Boston, riding a three-game winning streak, is 8-2 and sits a half-game behind Philadelphia.

"Another good opportunity for our team to be able to show what we're made of," Brown said. "I'm looking forward to it. Big-time matchup, two heavyweights in the East. Let's get it."

Brown has not hidden his appreciation for Boston, even as more has emerged about the deterioration of his relationship with Tatum and the organization.

"I love Boston," he said Sunday. "Boston's a great city. A lot of people I developed relationships with, you know what I mean? But I'm just focused on what's in front of me right now. And that's the most important thing."

He also acknowledged everything that accompanies an abrupt end to a decade with one organization.

"Decisions were made," Brown said. "It's life. It's business. Move on. It's OK. … It's emotions. You've got to pack up and move your stuff. Your family's got to pack up and move. But it's life. It's happened to numerous amounts of people, myself included.

"So I just look forward to being able to build with my team over here, trying to make the best of my situation."

Brown faced the Celtics during the preseason, but Tuesday will be the first meeting that counts. He said he did not know whether that would introduce emotions that were absent during the exhibition schedule.

"We'll see how I feel when we get out there," Brown said. "As of now, I've got no emotion toward nothing. So I'm just ready to go."

Tatum was similarly restrained after Boston's 117-97 victory over Milwaukee on Sunday.

"It's another game on the schedule," Tatum said. "It's the Celtics versus the Sixers. In the words of Brad Stevens, they all count as one."

Tatum called the Sixers "an incredible group" with a "great coaching staff," but declined to add to the discussion surrounding his relationship with Brown.

"Drama gets clicks and views," he said. "That's not my thing." CBS Sports reported Tatum's full comments Sunday night.

Celtics head coach Joe Mazzulla was even more concise.

"It'll be fun," Mazzulla said. "It'll be a good atmosphere."

Brown is not the only centerpiece of the July deal who will face his former team.

George has settled quickly into Boston's lineup, averaging 20.4 points and supplying the high-volume outside shooting the Celtics have long prioritized. He scored 27 points and made five three-pointers Sunday as Boston beat Milwaukee for its third consecutive victory.

The Celtics have not needed George to duplicate everything Brown provided. He has fit alongside Tatum, Derrick White and Boston's returning core as another willing shooter and versatile defender.

Tatum is averaging 27.1 points, six rebounds and 7.8 assists. His playmaking has been especially important for a team that replaced Brown's downhill pressure with more shooting and ball movement.

Boston enters Tuesday ranked third in offensive rating and fourth in defensive rating. It is one of two NBA teams currently inside the top five on both ends of the floor.

The Celtics have also made 15.6 three-pointers per game, the second-highest average in the league. They set an NBA Cup record by hitting 24 against Charlotte on Friday, then made 18 more Sunday in Milwaukee.

That presents a clear challenge for a Sixers defense that has generally performed well but has occasionally surrendered clean perimeter looks after committing extra help toward the ball. Tatum, George, White, Payton Pritchard and Sam Hauser can all punish late rotations.

The Sixers have reached 9-2 without depending upon the same formula every night.

Tyrese Maxey leads them at 26.9 points per game. Brown is averaging 22.3 points, 6.7 rebounds and four assists while shooting 47.7 percent from the field and 39.2 percent from three. Joel Embiid and LeBron James have supplied scoring, passing and defensive flexibility around them.

Brown has repeatedly described the team as unfinished despite its record.

"From our perspective, it's been some growing pains for sure, even though we've been able to win," he said. "The result, we're not so much fixated on the result. It's more so about the process, and we've got to improve our process and our mentality to creating a winning environment."

Boston should provide the clearest early measure of that process.

The Celtics play considerably slower than the Sixers, but their half-court execution has been more efficient. Philadelphia will want to create turnovers and run before Boston can set its defense. The Celtics will attempt to control the pace, generate three-pointers and make the Sixers execute against a defense that has yet to allow an opposing player to score 30 points this season.

There is also the unavoidable Brown matchup. Boston could use Tatum, George and White against him at different points, giving the Celtics several defenders capable of absorbing his drives without immediately sending help.

Nick Nurse expects Brown to bring something extra, even if Brown has publicly downplayed the emotional component.

"It's going to be very different for him," Nurse said. "I think probably a little bit of emotion in there, too, but also I think he's going to be very motivated to play."

Boston's rotation is healthy entering the matchup. Two-way guard Devin Carter is out because of an illness.

Dominick Barlow and Ariel Hukporti remain out for the Sixers. Adem Bona could return after missing Sunday's contest.

The betting market views the matchup as essentially a toss-up. Monday afternoon listings had the spread moving between Boston minus-1.5 and Philadelphia minus-1.5, depending on the sportsbook, with the total at 215.5.

That fits the larger picture. Philadelphia owns the East's best record. Boston has the conference's strongest point differential and has outscored opponents by 10.7 points per game.

The surrounding conversation will center on Brown and Tatum until the opening tip. Brown does not intend to contribute any ceremonial small talk.

"He's on his side," Brown said. "I'm on my side."

#2820. Iowa football in the NFL: Week 9 recap

Week 9 of the NFL season has wrapped up following Monday Night Football in Minneapolis.

On paper, this week's slate of games wasn't as appealing as usual. But as the NFL always seems to do, it delivered some classic games and big moments throughout the weekend. And of course, a few former Iowa football stars found a way to make their mark.

From first career touchdowns to savvy performances from veterans, here's a look at some of the best performances from former Hawkeyes in Week 9:

Johnson stepped up in a huge way for the Packers in their 45-27 win over the Patriots. He finished with 14 carries for 68 yards and a first-career NFL touchdown. He powered his way in from two yards out to pad Green Bay's lead in the second quarter. He also broke off a 34-yard run later in the game, showcasing the big-play ability Iowa fans grew to love.

Johnson's path to this point has been long. The Steelers cut him just a year after drafting him in the third round. Now he seems to have found a home in Green Bay. And with Josh Jacobs set to miss a lot of time with a foot injury, Johnson may only see his role increase in the coming weeks.

Kittle put together a solid day in the receiving game in the 49ers' 38-17 win over the Raiders. He finished with four receptions for 58 yards. But his biggest highlight of the day won't appear in the box score. Late in the third quarter, Kittle stayed in to block a deep shot to Jordan Watkins. He proceeded to handle multiple Raiders linemen, giving Brock Purdy enough time to find Watkins for a touchdown.

It's the small things that separate Kittle from the rest of the pack. He's a great receiver, but he has no problem doing the dirty work as well. There's a reason he's still one of the best tight ends in football.

Stevens did his job on Sunday, despite the Commanders dropping a 20-13 game to the Rams. He nailed both of his field goal attempts and his long extra-point attempt. After a slow start to the season, the rookie kicker is beginning to find his rhythm in Washington.

He's made 13 of 14 field goals since Week 2, including a career-long 57-yarder against Seattle. He's been perfect from inside 50 yards this season. Being a kicker in the NFL isn't easy. Stevens is showing he's up to the task in recent weeks.

#2819. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2818. The Number Crunch: Demolition Derby at Gillette Stadium

Not much to say about what happened on Sunday, other than a complete trouncing. The Patriots' had minimal push back in what was a rout of New England at the hands of the Green Bay Packers.

Let's go underneath the hood of the Patriots' Week 9 defeat.

New England's All-Pro cornerback's dreadful performance was the most glaring issue in this loss. Christian Gonzalez, the 24 year-old who just signed a record-setting four-year, $135 million deal, was the first one to tell the media he had a "bad game." But that might be an understatement, as this was his worst performance in his young NFL career.

According to NextGen Stats, Gonzalez allowed 10 receptions on 11 targets for 187 yards, which was easily the most he has ever given up in one game. In fact, Gonzalez nearly gave up more yardage in this game than in his first six combined this season, in which he allowed 196 yards.

He also unfortunately made history, becoming the first defender since at least 2018 to let up 80 or more yards to two receivers in the same game. Christian Watson (171 yards), and Matthew Golden (164 yards), each had career highs in receiving yards, combining for 335 of Jordan Love's 372 passing yards.

The Patriots have yet to win a game in which they force no turnovers in 2026, and are now 0-3 under that circumstance. New England has the exact same record when Drake Maye throws two or more interceptions in a game, tossing three more against Green Bay. His 11 interceptions through the first nine weeks of the NFL season, tied for the league lead alongside Justin Herbert, are the most for a Patriots' QB to open a season since Drew Bledsoe in 1994.

While he did pick up his play in the second half versus the Packers, the Patriots were already in a 17-point hole when he got the ball in the third quarter. His mistakes plus the defense allowing 31 points in the first 34 minutes and 9 seconds all contributed to the game's blowout nature.

A familiar limitation also showed itself again, adding to a game full of flaws on the New England side. Right when it felt that it was snowballing for the Patriots, it became an avalanche when Rhamondre Stevenson got stripped by Isaiah McDuffie midway through the third quarter. This is only Stevenson's second fumble on the year, the other one occurring in Week 2 against the Pittsburgh Steelers. In fact, his fumbling issues have gotten progressively better.

Since his alarming 2024 season, when he fumbled times, he only has five combined in 2025 and 2026. The second half begun with a Packers touchdown, followed by a Patriots punt, a Packers field goal, then the fumble by Stevenson. The Packers followed the turnover up with a one-play, 39 yard touchdown to Christian Watson, planting the momentum on the Green Bay sideline.

In three straight games, the Patriots have had to play from behind in the first half, having sluggish starts against the Bears, Dolphins, and now the Packers. New England began on Sunday down 17-0 after the celebration of the 2016 Patriots' Super Bowl team, not scoring their first points until 9:23 on the clock in the 2nd quarter. New England was also down 13-3 at halftime in Miami, and losing 10-0 at the conclusion of the first quarter up in Chicago. The Patriots have been outscored in the first half 51-37 in the past three games.

Patriots' head coach Mike Vrabel was asked about coming out skittish following the 45-27 loss to the Packers.

"It's tough to spot good teams any sort of lead," claimed Vrabel. "And we've done that."

The Patriots will have a long trip across the pond to Munich, Germany to figure out how to correct their numerous mistakes before they face the Detroit Lions. While the Lions' defense is not as strong as Green Bay's, their offense has fire power in Jahmyr Gibbs and Amon-Ra St. Brown. New England will need a strong gameplan to neutralize those two if they want to get back on track in Week 10.

#2816. Knicks 117, Bucks 85: Scenes from some spicy OG, KAT & Kolek cooking

One can draw conclusions from Milwaukee's 1-10 record. For instance, maybe Giannis Antetokounmpo was even more important to their success than we understood. Maybe we overestimated coach Taylor Jenkins a li'l bit? Or maybe the Deer just stink this season? We remember when Jenkins competed with Mike Brown for the Knicks' top job. There's a sliding doors moment we'd rather not consider.

Tonight, Jenkins got a solid effort from his team in the opening quarter. A regulation game lasts 48 minutes, though, and New York's stifling defense held the Bucks to just 34 points through the first 24. Ahead by two dozen at halftime, the home team let the guests cut the deficit to 11 early in the fourth. Then the dynamic duo of Karl-Anthony Towns and Tyler Kolek restored some sanity, and by the bell, the Knicks had won, 117–85.

For those counting at home, that's four consecutive wins to bring New York to 6-4. It's also the fourth time in five games that they've held an opponent under 100 points. No Knicks starter played more than 30 minutes (nice, because they'll need their legs tomorrow against Cleveland). And that's officially the Bucks' worst start in 33 years.

The Bucks didn't play like a last-place team in the first quarter, nor did the Knicks comport themselves like world champions. The lead changed seven times before New York finally found traction late in the quarter. On the plus side, Landry Shamet hit two treys, and Mikal Bridges chipped in two buckets, three rebounds, a steal and a block. For the villains, Ryan Rollins stood out with five points, two assists, two rebounds and a steal.

The Knicks lead their conference in rebounding percentage (53.3%). Give credit to Karl-Anthony Towns (21 PTS, 16 RBS, 27 MIN), who's grabbing 13.6 boards per night. The KAT campaign for MVP rolled on this evening. In the first quarter, Towns played straight-up defense on one end, deterring a Ryan Rollins drive. At the other end, he played quarterback, hitting Jalen Brunson (23 PTS) on a cut to the cup. He feasted on the well-fed Myles Turner, and the Knicks won the paint 16-6 and the boards 16-8 in the first quarter. The three-ball kept the Bucks competitive, but by the period's end, the hosts were ahead 26-21.

From the "Gotta Complain About Something" file, Anunoby failed to flush the ball twice tonight. He's getting to the charity stripe at a career-high clip, but he's also clanged plenty off the front of the iron. You can't gripe about this stat-line, though: 19 points, 6-of-12 FG, 5-of-8 3PT, and a +26.

After a strong showing in the first quarter, the visitors wilted fast in the second. Milwaukee scored first, then New York rattled off 10 straight to push the lead to a baker's dozen. Tyler Kolek (12 PTS, 2-3 3PT, 15 MIN) capped the run with a three, then blocked an AJ Green three, grabbed the rebound and took it the other way for a layup. After a Jenkins timeout, Kolek turned a missed Jaime Jaquez, Jr. (16 PTS) three into a driving finger roll for two.

A little past midway through the frame, the good guys had outscored Milwaukee 19-5. Towns cleaned the glass, and Andre Drummond (14 RBS, 16 MIN) grabbed nine boards in eight first-half minutes. Three came on the offensive end.

Anunoby had a fine second frame, scoring eight with two threes, two assists and a steal. Brunson chipped in six points, and Towns grabbed five boards and made two free throws. Nate Ament led Milwaukee with five points and added a steal. The Knicks won the frame 32-13 and went into halftime up 58-34.

At the half, Milwaukee had matched New York's eight threes and nearly matched its percentage from deep (35% to 36%). Inside the arc, the Bucks were a dreadful 5-for-19 against the Knicks' 14-for-27. New York also doubled Milwaukee on the boards, 34-17, including 10 offensive rebounds. Anunoby, Brunson and Shamet shared the halftime scoring lead with 10 apiece. Ament had seven for the guests.

Jaquez burst out of intermission with fire and desire, scoring nine of his team's first 15 points as the Bucks lopped 10 points off their deficit. Rollins had a fine quarter, too, hitting three treys. KAT missed his first six tries from deep but still managed his 10th double-double of the season. The Captain found his touch, scoring 13 of the Knicks' 27 third-quarter points. For the visitors, Kasparas Jakučionis added five points and two assists in the final 3:08. The Bucks won the quarter 38-27 and trailed 85-72 heading into the fourth.

After surrendering an alley-oop to Kel'el Ware, the Knicks found themselves ahead by 11 early in the fourth. It was time to get serious. Towns drilled a triple, his first make in seven tries. He missed two freebies, but Shamet grabbed the rebound and Kolek cashed it in from deep. Oh, and don't look now, but here comes Karl with another bomb! He and Kolek synergized and synthesized, combining for New York's first 11 points of the quarter.

Anunoby buried back-to-back threes to cap a 21-6 Knicks run that stretched the lead from 13 to 28. Gary Trent Jr. scored five quick points for Milwaukee, but it was too little, too late. Dalton Knecht, Pacôme Dadiet and Jose Alvarado got some run as the bench crew swept up and turned off the lights while the starters thought about their dinner plans.

Mo is basting his recap in delicious juices. The Knicks are back at the Garden tomorrow for another clash with the Cavs. Rest up, Knickerbockers.

#2815. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

#2814. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

#2813. Rams more likely to miss the playoffs than make the playoffs, according to betting odds

The Rams entered the season as the overwhelming favorites to win the Super Bowl. As the dust settles on the NFL trade deadline, the Rams are now not even favored to make the playoffs.

The latest betting odds have the Rams as -130 favorites to miss the playoffs, and +110 underdogs to make the playoffs.

That represents a massive shift from before the season, when the Rams were -500 favorites to make the playoffs, the best playoff odds of any team in the league.

The Rams are a disappointing 5-4, and if the playoffs started today they'd be a game behind the Packers for the third and final NFC playoff spot. They're also 2.5 games behind the 49ers in the NFC West race.

Perhaps more importantly, injuries are mounting for the Rams, with the latest big one being that wide receiver Puka Nacua is heading to injured reserve, where he'll join Davante Adams, as Matthew Stafford is running out of healthy receivers.

If the Rams get healthy and make the playoffs, they'll be a major threat to any postseason opponent, which is why they still have the seventh-best odds in the league to win the Super Bowl at +1300.

But as the 49ers pull away in the NFC West, and the NFC wild card race looks increasingly competitive, the Rams' chances of being in it in January are dwindling.

#2812. Red Wings Could Face a Massive Decision on Reported Larkin Package

The Detroit Red Wings have a massive decision to make regarding Dylan Larkin, and the latest trade speculation involving the Minnesota Wild is only making that decision more complicated. With a potential package involving a promising young forward and three first-round draft picks being discussed, interim general manager Shawn Horcoff must determine whether Detroit can afford to pass on such an opportunity.

According to a November 10 report from Detroit Hockey Insider, NHL insider Elliotte Friedman believes Minnesota's potential offer includes Danila Yurov and three first-round selections. However, those exact terms have not been confirmed by either organization, and Friedman previously indicated uncertainty about the number of picks involved.

For a Red Wings organization desperate to build a sustainable winner, that is a significant amount of potential future talent. But is it enough to justify trading one of Detroit's most accomplished players?

Oct 24, 2026; Calgary, Alberta, CAN; Detroit Red Wings center Dylan Larkin (71) skates with the puck against the Calgary Flames during the second period at Scotiabank Saddledome. Mandatory Credit: Sergei Belski-Imagn Images Credit: IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect (Reuters)

Minnesota has repeatedly surfaced as one of the most aggressive teams pursuing Larkin, and the reported trade possibilities have continued to attract attention around the NHL. The Wild appear interested in acquiring an established center capable of strengthening their lineup, while Detroit remains determined to secure an appropriate return.

Yurov, a 22-year-old forward selected 24th overall in the 2022 NHL Draft, would provide Detroit with another young offensive player. Adding three first-round selections could also give the Red Wings considerable flexibility to acquire future talent or potentially pursue established players through additional trades.

However, there is an important distinction between receiving valuable assets and receiving players who can immediately make Detroit better.

Horcoff has already made his intentions clear regarding the type of return he wants, emphasizing the importance of acquiring players who can contribute now. As the organization continues to evaluate its asking price in a potential Larkin trade, the difference between immediate help and future assets could ultimately determine whether a deal gets completed.

The biggest problem for Detroit is that Larkin still has considerable control over where he can be traded. His approved list reportedly contains 10 teams, which limits Horcoff's ability to create competition among interested organizations.

According to NHL.com's Nicholas J. Cotsonika, Horcoff has expressed his willingness to wait until the Red Wings receive a return that benefits the organization. That position was reinforced Tuesday when the interim general manager indicated that the situation remained unchanged.

Detroit's ongoing trade standoff with Larkin has created an uncomfortable situation for everyone involved. The former captain remains suspended without pay, and there has been no indication that a resolution is imminent.

That brings us back to Minnesota's potential package.

If Yurov and three first-round picks are genuinely available, Horcoff must decide whether that represents the best return Detroit can realistically obtain. Holding out for more established NHL talent might be understandable, but there is also a risk that an interested team eventually decides to pursue another option.

There is another possibility worth considering. Detroit could potentially use some of those draft selections in subsequent deals to acquire players who are ready to contribute immediately, effectively transforming a futures-heavy package into a more balanced return.

Of course, that strategy comes with its own risks, particularly if the Red Wings struggle to convert those assets into meaningful improvements.

The Red Wings cannot afford to rush this decision simply because the Larkin situation has become uncomfortable. At the same time, they cannot afford to ignore a substantial return if it presents the best opportunity to improve the franchise.

Three first-round picks and a young player with upside would certainly deserve serious consideration. Whether that package ultimately meets Detroit's needs is another question entirely, especially given the organization's desire to become competitive sooner rather than later.

For now, there is no confirmed agreement between the Red Wings and Wild, and Friedman's assessment should not be mistaken for a finalized trade offer. But the possibility of such a return raises an important question for Horcoff and the entire organization.

Would you trade Dylan Larkin for Danila Yurov and three first-round picks, or should the Red Wings continue holding out for more?

#2810. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

Good morning, New York Giants fans! Here are the stories you need to on Veteran's Day 2026.

On a short week like this, it's all hands-on deck. Everybody, all the time, every second, get ready for this game because it comes fast, but that's how you get prepared."

"You've seen what I can do," Skattebo said. "When I come out and I'm ready to play football, I can be one of the most dangerous backs in the league. Simple as that."

Those adjustments have been intentional, mixing blitzes, simulated pressure, two-deep and three-deep looks, man coverage, and zone. The players have applied the changes effectively, even on days when opponents have moved the ball.

"I think he's done a great job with that. I mean, to me that's one of the important things in coaching, a mark of a great coach, is the ability to adapt to your personnel, your circumstances, the opponent, all those things, and have a system built that's able to do that," Harbaugh said. "And Dennard and the whole coaching staff have done a great job of that."

The biggest question surrounding Reese during the build-up to the NFL Draft was "Where do you play him?" The answer should have been another question: "Why does it matter?" And the Giants were exactly right to take that approach. Reese was an EDGE at Ohio State who moonlighted at off-ball linebacker. When the Giants initially drafted him, John Harbaugh basically said he'd play linebacker but also move around. Through eight games, Reese has now played at least 30 snaps at seven different spots: right inside linebacker, left inside linebacker, right linebacker, right EDGE, left linebacker, middle linebacker and left EDGE.

Here's where the self-awareness portion comes in. The Giants knew neither they nor Reese had to have it figured out on Day 1. The Giants are in Year 1 under John Harbaugh and defensive coordinator Dennard Wilson. Reese is 21 years old. The world was (and still is!) their oyster to experiment. Reese looks phenomenal. The Giants are 5-2-1 despite having started three different quarterbacks, and players such as Reese give them a bright future, too.

D.J. Reader is not supposed to be playing this well.

Maybe it's the gray showing through his beard, but it's gotten relatively obvious: the New York Giants defensive lineman isn't out to hide the fact that, in NFL terms, he's one of the "old heads" in the locker room. If anything, the 32-year-old Reader sees his age as a super power.

New York Giants: Dl D.J. Reader (90.0). New York's defense is chock full of impressive youngsters, but Reader has taken the cake through nine games. His 90.0 overall PFF grade leads all qualified interior defenders this season despite signing for a relatively pedestrian contract in the offseason. Believe it or not, Reader is the only player at the position with an 80.0-plus PFF run-defense and pass-rush grade.

#2805. Five takeaways from UNC basketball taking down Wyoming on Tuesday

No basketball game is ever going to be perfect. Just ask the North Carolina Tar Heels, who welcomed Wyoming to the Dean Dome on Tuesday night.

UNC led the Cowboys by just two points at halftime, but whatever Michael Malone said during the break, it worked. North Carolina looked a much different team in the second half, out-scoring the Cowboys by 11 en route to a 76-63 victory.

The Tar Heels (3-0) didn't allow Wyoming (2-1) to control the offensive glass in second-half action, despite finishing the game with a 19-10 disadvantage. UNC's defense turned in another strong performance, holding the visiting Cowboys to 36 percent from the field, including a 26% mark from deep. Wyoming leading scorer Nasir Meyer shot 8-of-21, while Uriyah Rojas made just two shots in 10 attempts off the bench.

North Carolina doesn't have a go-to scorer, but players across the squad who can drop 10 points on any given night. Terrence Brown and Isaiah Denis led the Tar Heels with 14 points each on Tuesday night, Maximo Adams and Sayon Keita added 13 points apiece, while Matt Able capped the double-digit scoring attack with 11.

Keita looked every bit of the dominant paint presence UNC needs, completing his night with eight rebounds and six blocks. Give Keita another 20 minutes, he completes a rare triple-double.

You can be upset about North Carolina's continued struggles on the glass, or you can be happy about the several additional positives. Read our top five takeaways from tonight's big win.

Every good team has a strong big man. Through three preseason exhibitions and a trio of regular season games, Keita is living up to his hype.

Keita is more of a defensive-minded center, grabbing 14 rebounds and block seven shots in three November matchups. Keita's 7'1" frame makes opponent re-adjust their shots, while he can also defend the perimeter, but thunderous dunks are becoming common.

Any team craves for a scorer like RJ Davis, but there's benefits to having a more balanced scoring attack. UNC's 2026-27 roster has a plethora of scorers, mainly in its backcourt.

Denis and Adams totaled 27 points on Tuesday...off North Carolina's bench. Able, Brown and Keita combined for 38 in the starting lineup. Jarin Stevenson can also score in bunches.

As the Tar Heels play lesser competition early in non-conference play, they won't have to worry about rebounding as much. When UNC transitions into a tough December and later, ACC play, rebounding will become an issue.

North Carolina lost the offensive rebound battle in each of its first three games, including a 19-10 disadvantage on Tuesday. The Tar Heels have height on their roster, but guys not named Keita and Stevenson need to produce.

Malone is a no-nonsense, shoot you straight coach who loves his players. People loved Hubert Davis, but he gave off a nice guy mentality who sugarcoated things.

North Carolina needed a coaching change in the worst way. Malone may not have prior collegiate head-coaching experience, but he has a winning pedigree across 12 NBA seasons.

Just three games in, the Tar Heels already love their new coach. He knows how to win at both levels and is bringing a fresh, winning perspective to Chapel Hill.

Blowing out Wofford was a welcome result. Beating Western Carolina by eight proved concerning, but a 13-point win against Wyoming is expected.

If North Carolina keeps winning, the final score doesn't matter. The Tar Heels will be challenged more against Georgia on Friday, but Malone will have his guys prepared.
