#2804. No Bull thoughts on the ILB room after the Daiyan Henley trade

I've been hammering the Broncos on their reluctance to upgrade the inside linebacker position for years. No serious draft picks spent on it, no trades, and no serious free agent pursuit.

One of the most overlooked reasons that our 2015 defense was so dominant was the quality of talent we had at ILB. We had Danny Trevathan and Brandon Marshall. Both of those guys could cover. Both defended the run. They were playmakers just like everyone else on that defense.

The Broncos signing Daiyan Henley is an A+ move, and I darn near would give them the same grade if they had traded a 3rd-round pick for him.

Henley is a player that has been loved and respected in the Chargers' locker room. He's a captain of the team. He's also a guy who has worn the green dot for the defense (calling the plays for the defense). This is yet another high-character player added to a locker room built on high character and positive culture.

Athletically, he's the most talented linebacker on the inside we have seen in a very long time. His capability at coverage is excellent. He's also a more sure tackler than either of our current starters.

So the question for this team becomes: what happens with our defense?

While I think he's a real option for the future captain of our defense over Alex Singleton, that day will not be soon. He's going to have to learn the defense and be in the system a while before the Broncos can even think of that.

I expect him to be in the mix in nickel downs coming out of the bye. I think he's likely going to take Justin Strnad's spot as the starter before the season is over.

No matter what happens with the snap count, having depth at inside linebacker is a big deal. Having a linebacker who can cover running backs in the flat and tight ends could very well be the missing piece for this defense to take a big step forward.

#2802. Malik Willis going on IR can cost the Packers a round in the 2027 draft

The NFL's compensatory pick formula is a little complicated, but to boil it down: Players are awarded points for their average salary over the life of the contract that they signed in free agency and receive playing-time bonuses based on the percentage of snaps they play during the regular season. If you lose players worth more points than the ones you sign, you get a draft pick from the NFL (which essentially mints an extra round of the draft, awarding 32 picks from the third to seventh rounds).

That's why the news of Malik Willis, a former Green Bay Packers quarterback, going on injured reserve impacts Green Bay in 2027. Already, Over The Cap has adjusted Willis' compensatory draft pick projection to be a fourth-round pick, rather than the third-round pick he was initially expected to earn for the Packers. Willis was already a borderline third-round case (there are only two players currently set to earn third-round compensatory picks: edge defenders Jaelen Phillips and Trey Hendrickson), but losing at least four games of action is essentially a nail in the coffin for that selection.

So instead of having what was projected to be the 98th pick in the draft for the loss of Willis, the Packers are now projected to receive the 133rd pick, according to Over The Cap.

Former Packers leaving Green Bay haven't had much luck this year. Offensive tackle Rasheed Walker, who was expected to net the Packers at least a fifth-round pick in free agency, overplayed his hand and signed late — leaving him with just a one-year deal. At the moment, the Packers are not expected to be awarded a pick for the loss of Walker. On top of that, receiver Romeo Doubs, who had a chance to earn Green Bay a fourth-round pick, is also on the injured reserve, which likely means that the Packers will only get a fifth-round pick for losing him.

Overall, here's the breakdown of the current outlook for the Packers' compensatory picks in 2027:

Teams are capped at four compensatory draft picks, or Walker (seventh round) would have been in play — had the team not canceled out that selection with the addition of cornerback Benjamin St-Juste in free agency.

As a reminder, the Packers own their original draft picks outside of the first (Micah Parsons trade) and sixth (Darian Kinnard trade) rounds. They will also get the Cowboys' fourth-round pick (Rashan Gary trade) and the Eagles' sixth-round pick (Dontayvion Wicks trade) in 2027, bringing their overall total to 11 picks.

#2800. Stephen A. Smith skeptical of Lakers’ title chances because top three players are white

This July, the Los Angeles Lakers closed the LeBron James chapter—well, LeBron James chose to go to Philadelphia—and officially ushered in the Luka Dončić era.

The Lakers acquired center Walker Kessler from the Utah Jazz and re-signed Austin Reaves, forming a Big 3, if you dare call it that, of Dončić, Kessler, and Reaves.

At the time, Stephen A. Smith dubbed the new-look Lakers "White Dude Central," which predictably drew strong reactions across sports media.

Los Angeles is off to a scorching 10-2 start to the 2026-27 NBA season, led by Dončić averaging a league-best 39.3 points per game. Despite that, Smith remains skeptical.

During Tuesday's episode of The Stephen A. Smith Show on SiriusXM, a Floridian named Andrew called in and expressed his shock at how white the Lakers are before asking for Smith's opinion on the team's legitimacy.

"Let me say a couple of things. No. 1, I'm always rooting for the Lakers, never against them, because I love being in L.A. I just don't want to live there because of Gavin Newsom and the high damn taxes," said Smith, who can't resist political punditry these days. "But I love L.A. No. 2, Luka Doncic is special. Right now, if we were to vote on a league MVP, he would win it."

Smith similarly lauded Lakers head coach JJ Redick and new franchise ownership led by Josh Kushner and Bob Iger before placing a hard ceiling on this season's title hopes in Los Angeles.

"The bottom line is that I think the Lakers are in good hands, and the future is gonna be incredibly, incredibly bright," Smith said. "But with that being said, I do not think that they're getting past the second round. I'd be shocked if they did. I am on the record being controversially because I've never seen an NBA team with its top players being white deliver a championship. It's never happened. And I'm here to tell you I don't think it's going to happen this year."

Smith left the door open for Los Angeles, given the Oklahoma City Thunder's injuries and the Denver Nuggets' slow start, but he held firm that the Lakers won't win the championship this season.

Andrew, the caller, clarified that he doesn't care about race and didn't want to come across as "the white guy calling about the white team."

"Listen, I'm not caring about it, either," Smith said. "I'm just talking about logistics—I'm just talking about the actual facts."

Smith rattled off a litany of dynastic and historic NBA teams and each one's makeup of stars before reiterating his point.

"You can bring up whoever the hell you want," Smith said. "There has never been, in my lifetime, an NBA team led with its top three players being white that delivered you a championship when an abundance of brothers [are] playing basketball. That hasn't happened. And I'm here to tell you it ain't gonna happen. I can't see it coming."

"I understand that there are people who think otherwise, who wish otherwise. The Olympics are coming to L.A. in 2028. Could you imagine, as the world stage, considering the global, iconic brand that the NBA is, if Luka Doncic somehow managed to win an NBA championship? That would be huge for the league. It would be huge for the Olympics; it would be huge for L.A.; it would be huge for everything. But it ain't gonna happen. I can't see it.

"Not a team with its top three players being white dudes. I'm sorry. I think that's controversial in some people's eyes. It ain't controversial in mine. I'm just telling you how it is. I'm calling it like I see it: I have never in my lifetime seen an NBA team with its top three players being white win a championship. It has never happened, and it ain't gonna happen."

An awkward beat passed before we all realized Andrew from Florida was still on the line. "Hey, if it does, it does, if it doesn't, it doesn't," Andrew said. "Either way, we're just gonna watch."

Now that's the hard-hitting sports analysis we can all get behind.

The post Stephen A. Smith skeptical of Lakers' title chances because top three players are white appeared first on Awful Announcing.

#2799. Dolphins place QB Malik Willis on IR with core muscle injury

The Dolphins placed quarterback Malik Willis on injured reserve, the team announced.

Willis has had a groin injury that has limited his mobility, and an MRI showed it developed into a core muscle injury, Tom Pelissero of The Ringer reports.

Pelissero said the injury is not season-ending.

The Dolphins have Kyle McCord behind Willis, along with Brady Cook on the practice squad. McCord, a sixth-round pick of the Eagles in 2025, has never seen action in a regular-season game.

He presumably will start Sunday’s game against the Colts.

Willis will miss at least four games before returning.

He has a 58.2 percent completion percentage for 1,396 yards with six touchdowns and six interceptions.

The Dolphins also announced they placed defensive lineman Zach Sieler on injured reserve and waived cornerback Reese Taylor.

#2798. Knicks thrash woeful Bucks en route to fourth consecutive win

They stepped on the throats of an overmatched opponent early Tuesday night and didn't let go of the squeeze, pummeling the Bucks 117-85 at MSG.

It represented a fourth straight win for the Knicks (6-4), whose 0-3 stumble to start their title defense is firmly in the rearview.

Karl-Anthony Towns, who scored 21 points and grabbed 16 rebounds, goes up for a shot during the Knicks' 117-85 blowout win over the Bucks on Nov. 10, 2026 at the Garden. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

Jalen Brunson led the scoring with 23 points on 18 shots and Karl-Anthony Towns grabbed 16 boards with 21 points. It was Towns' 10th straight double-double to start the season, one off the franchise record set by Willis Reed in 1970.

"He continues to be a double-double machine at a high level," coach Mike Brown.

The Knicks benefited from a balanced attack, with OG Anunoby adding 19 points in 29 minutes.

But again, the Knicks rode their stout defense. They held the sad-sack Bucks (1-10) to 38 percent shooting. Their past five opponents have all scored fewer than 100 points, a rare streak in the 3-point era.

Jalen Brunson, who scored a game-high 23 points, goes up for a layup as Kyle Kuzma defends during the Knicks' win over the Bucks. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

"I just think that we're doing a great job of getting back to the basics. We started off slow, and now we're picking it up," said Towns, who was down on himself after the game for shooting 2-for-9 on treys. "While the shooting isn't doing great —

I could be the first one to tell you about that tonight — but I think we're doing a great job of executing right now at a high level, and our defense is there. At the end of the day, the offense will catch up. The defense is something you got to establish. We're doing a great job of that right now."

The game felt over by halftime, when the Knicks were up by 24 and the Bucks had only 34 points. The Knicks led the rebounding battle 34-17 at the break and ended the night with a staggering 70 boards. Milwaukee cut the deficit to 13 in the third quarter but never got it into single digits in the second half. The Knicks stomped Milwaukee 32-13 in the fourth quarter.

OG Anunoby, who scored 19 points, shoots a jumper during the Knicks' win over the Bucks. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

"Our group, they were pretty efficient tonight," Brown said. "I thought our physicality on the defensive end of the floor was at a pretty high level, and they did it the right way. They did it without fouling. Which was a lot of fun to watch. The point-of-attack defense, with Landry [Shamet] and especially Deuce [McBride] on the ball, was unbelievable. Deuce was our defensive player of the game. Could have gone to Landry as well. But just a fantastic job at the point of the screen in pick-and-rolls. [The Bucks] had two 13-point quarters and I'm sure they missed some shots, but I thought we also impacted their shots with our play."

The Knicks were without Josh Hart for a third consecutive game. He was out with an illness after missing two games because of ankle soreness. Shamet again started in Hart's spot and scored 10 points in 28 minutes.

New York also got solid bench contributions. Tyler Kolek continued to play over Jose Alvarado and finished with 12 points, four assists and a block. During Kolek's 15 minutes, the Knicks outscored the Bucks by 23. They were actually better with McBride on the floor. The backup guard was a plus-39 in 25 minutes.

New York Knicks center Andre Drummond passes to guard Jalen Brunson during the second quarter. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

Andre Drummond grabbed 14 rebounds in 16 minutes.

The Bucks are a disaster this season after trading Giannis Antetokounmpo to the Miami Heat. It didn't help Tuesday that their leading scorer — Tyler Herro — missed the game because of a knee contusion. Myles Turner, who is in the middle of a $109 million contract, was a lazy disaster with three points on 1-for-7 shooting while getting roasted on defense by Towns.

#2793. Celtics’ red-hot shooting, PG’s big night overshadow Brown-Tatum matchup in Sixers’ loss

The high-profile, max contract wing dropped 36 points to lead his new team to a win over his old team, just like everyone expected.

The Sixers were demolished 123-101 by the Boston Celtics thanks to a Paul George masterclass Tuesday night.

In his first game back against his old team, Jaylen Brown didn't have the night he envisioned, putting up just 13 shooting 4-of-9 from the field, sitting for much of the second half in foul trouble.

Tyrese Maxey was the entire Sixers' offense for too much of the night, leading the team with 24 on 8-of-12 shooting with four assists. Joel Embiid was able to get to the line but still struggled from the floor, finishing with 16 points, six rebounds and one assist shooting 2-of-9 from the floor.

LeBron James struggled as well, having 11 points on 4-of-12 shooting along with eight rebounds and five assists. George led all scorers with his 36.

The Sixers were once again without Adem Bona (neck) and Dominick Barlow (back).

Revenge was had on the first bucket of the game as Paul George was able to drive through Maxey for a midrange pull-up, just like everyone anticipated. He did the same over VJ Edgecombe a couple possessions later, but did foul Embiid to help the Sixers' score their first points of the game.

Brown's first shot against his old team was an open corner three swung to him that didn't fall. When he got Jayson Tatum matched up on him, the crowd came to a roar, but Tatum won the first round as he was able to force a turnover with the help of a double team. As a team the Sixers had some sloppiness to start the game. Embiid had an ugly turnover trying to throw a behind-the-back pass, and they completely lost Tatum on a backdoor cut for an open layup.

Maxey got off to a tremendous start, one that the Sixers very much needed as the rest of the starters opened the game 0-of-5 from the floor. His shotmaking from all over the floor prevented the Sixers from going down into a big hole early. Desperate to make something happen, Brown did a good job to steal an outlet pass, but Derrick White was able to slide under and take a charge to discount the basket. He finally made his first field goal of the game driving by Jordan Walsh to slam it home.

While he didn't hit a jumper to break the seal, the Sixers offense came alive more with Embiid getting to the line. It's clear he's still trying to find that trust in his jumper again, but the way he was getting fouled in the post made him look a bit more like himself. VJ Edgecombe made a brilliant spin move to cut the lead to three.

That bucket was followed by Embiid's second turnover of the game, a contested Maxey fadeaway, and Embiid missing two out of three from the free throw line. Maxey tried to hit Dean Wade in the dunker finishing a drive, but Wade wasn't ready for it and dropped it out of bounds. Anfernee Simons hit a pull-up jumper to end a field goal drought that lasted over four and a half minutes and cut the Sixers' deficit to seven points after the first.

The end of the first and beginning of the second made clear the Sixers were leaning more into staggering Brown and James together, leaving the three incumbent starters together. LeBron was also scoreless in the first and hit a midrange early in the second to get on the board. He also found Simons in the corner from three, but with the Celtics scoring on four of their first five possessions of the quarter the Sixers continued to fall behind.

Nothing emphasized the Sixers' struggles to get anything to fall like their struggles around the basket. LeBron smoked his second bunny of the game, then tried to hit Jabari Walker right by the basket, but Walker couldn't corral it and lost the ball out of bounds. They started the game shooting 44% at the rim, which Cleaning the Glass put in just the third percentile across the league.

After a rare made basket, the Sixers were too slow getting back and conceded an open three to Sam Hauser before anyone had gotten back. Brown missing a pair of free throws followed by Hauser hitting a very contested three off the dribble made this game feel like it was starting to spiral. Coming out of a timeout, George beat Brown for an offensive rebound and drew a foul as he banked in his putback.

The combination of this game's struggles at the rim and the season's struggles in transition all came to a head on one play. LeBron hit Embiid with a touchdown pass and Embiid took off a little too early and missed the layup, and got his feet tangled up with George as they landed. Edgecombe was able to find the loose ball and slam it back home, but Embiid stayed on the ground for a few moments and hobbled his way through the final 30 seconds of the half. The Sixers trailed by 11 at the break.

Despite that scare, Embiid was not only out there to start the second half, but he started it by making his first three-point attempt since the home opener. Maxey was able to get to the line on the Sixers next possession, but the Celtics halted any sort of momentum right there. Tatum hit a contested three over Maxey before Brown picked up his fourth foul of the night.

LeBron was also desperate for a jumper to fall and it took him two attempts in the third to finally get a three to go down. Edgecombe, who was easily one of the best Sixers on the floor so far, followed that up with a very acrobatic finish over Neemias Queta. Maxey was able to get to the basket on the next possession, cutting the lead to six and prompting a Celtics timeout.

The Sixers continued to mount momentum coming out of the timeout. Embiid was still able to get to the line and LeBron started getting downhill as well. They did a good job of not letting big Celtics baskets kill their momentum. Tatum got LeBron pretty good with a dunk and George buried another three, but the Sixers kept eating into the lead.

Wade was putting in a great night for the Sixers, and it helped that he got some threes up and buried a couple, one of which briefly tied the game up. Maxey hit a ridiculous three to keep it tied for a second, but Boston had another onslaught of their own to go back in front. One hit off the top of the backboard, but it also didn't help that the Sixers kept leaving Hauser and Tatum wide open on the perimeter. Wade had an open three rim out to keep the Celtics up by nine.

The Celtics drilled three more triples before the Sixers got on the board to start the quarter, prompting a timeout from Nick Nurse. The full starting five checked back into the game much earlier than normal in one last attempt to salvage the night. The first basket coming out of that timeout was George posting up Maxey, hitting his pull-up while getting fouled in the process.

George scooted past the entire Sixers defense for a layup on the following possession. Maxey appeared to have earned a trip to the line for the Sixers first points of the quarter, but Joe Mazzulla challenged the play and got it overturned. The Sixers didn't score their first points of the fourth until the 7:37 mark when a Brown three bounced in. The Sixers waived the white flag after a couple more minutes of George burying threes on them.

#2792. Bengals News: Embarrassing Trade Deadline for Cincinnati

The 4 PM NFL trade deadline came and went on Tuesday, and the Cincinnati Bengals didn't make a single addition to their team.

They tried to send a sixth-round pick to the Eagles in exchange for edge rusher Arnold Ebiketie, a move most would have celebrated. At least at first.

They backed out, though, after they learned—likely on X from OverTheCap.com's Nick Korte and not even someone from inside their own building—they'd lose the third-round compensatory pick they earned in the '27 draft if the trade went through.

We learned the Bengals likely don't even have anyone on the staff that could foresee how trading for a guy who was signed as a compensatory free agent could affect their future. We also learned the defense that has shown flashes of really good and flashes of really bad is the one Joe Burrow and the offense is going to be tethered to for the entire season.

Bengals Face Harsh Reality After NFL Trade Deadline

People are going to put a lot of blame on the Bengals and their front office for not making a swing. In some ways, it will be unfair. In other ways, they have built up a reputation of not going all-in when they have to.

How Bengals' top opponents in the AFC handled Tuesday's NFL trade deadline

There wasn't a more aggressive team in the AFC than the Jacksonville Jaguars, however, who added three veterans before the deadline. Former Bengals safety Jessie Bates III came over from Atlanta, defensive tackle Jordan Davis was dealt from the Eagles to the Jaguars and defensive tackle Davon Godchaux arrived in a trade with the New Orleans Saints.

Hidden strength of Bengals' roster just took a hit and there was nothing they could've done to stop it

The Bengals were lucky to bring Johnson back on their practice squad. Having both him and Flacco behind Burrow was such a luxury, and made the QB room arguably the best in the league. Even if Johnson wasn't on the active roster, his presence in the building and experience of nearly 20 years playing professional ball were valuable on their own.

#2791. Cam Skattebo on Giants not adding a running back: They know what I can do

Cam Skattebo is set to return to the Giants lineup for Thursday night’s game against the Commanders and he won’t be looking over his shoulder at any new additions to the team’s running back depth chart.

The Giants were reportedly bidders for Jonathan Taylor at one point and they were linked to other potential moves in the backfield, but they stood pat on deadline day. Skattebo said on Tuesday that he understands why some would be “skeptical” that the Giants have enough at running back while citing his average of around 60 rushing yards per game, but that he wasn’t bothered by any interest the Giants had in other backs because “they know what I can do”

“You’ve seen what I can do,” Skattebo said, via Darryl Slater of NJ.com. “When I come out and I’m ready to play football, I can be one of the most dangerous backs in the league. Simple as that.”

Skattebo was up to full practice participation Tuesday after missing Week 9 with a hamstring injury, so he should have every opportunity to prove himself right about what he’s capable of doing for the Giants offense.

#2790. The glaring issue holding back Nets as team rallies together to turnaround slow start

DALLAS — When Benjamin Franklin said, "We must all hang together, or assuredly we shall all hang separately," the sentiment was about building a country.

The Nets are finding that out the hard way.

They enter Wednesday's tilt in New Orleans a disheartening 2-8. And with a rare road practice the day before in Dallas, the Nets went in intent on keeping the morale up, the energy positive and — most importantly — the team together.

Julius Randle drives on Jalen Johnson on during the Nets' loss to the Hawks on Nov. 7, 2026 in Atlanta. Getty Images

"That was one of my points after the [Dallas] game; it's what we need to not only get better, but also come together," Jordi Fernández said. "Our practice, the energy was outstanding and we got better. So right now we want to go out there and play a good brand of basketball."

The Nets had played a bad brand of ball in their 130-93 loss to the Mavericks, a desultory defeat in which they were disjointed and disconnected.

Getting on the same page was a main point of emphasis in their practice, as was getting in a good headspace.

"There's a lot of things to be down about. But it's a long season, and if we get down already, then it's going to be a longer season," admitted Michael Porter Jr. "So coming here with good energy and get better was what we needed to do."

The proof will be in the pudding, or against the Pelicans.

Brooklyn was disorganized offensively against the Mavericks, with 21 turnovers resulting in 25 points.

It's a recurring theme after coughing up 22 giveaways for 28 points the night before in a heartbreaking 124-121 loss in Atlanta.

"Yeah, it's pretty simple, right? I know the guys are not trying to turn it over, so put less pressure on yourself when you make those decisions," said Fernández. "It's more about our spacing and then how we do from there because we're pretty unorganized early and then that doesn't help. So I don't want them to play tentative; I want them to play aggressive, and making their decisions and playing very hard.

"And then defensively it's going back to what is going to make us be a very good defensive team is ball pressure first. Build our trust through communication, our coverages, and then finish with multiple efforts. We've shown it at times, and now in this next set of five games we want to see improvement."

The turnover issue has been death by a thousand cuts. And worst of all, those slices are self-inflicted wounds.

Shoddy spacing, bad reads and similar gaffes lead to giveaways.

Brooklyn began Tuesday third worst in the league in turnovers and turnover percentage, and fourth worst in points off those giveaways.

Michael Porter Jr. handles the ball during the Nets' blowout loss to the Mavericks on Nov. 8, 2026 in Dallas. Getty Images

"That's what we're going over, so that's all I know," said Day'Ron Sharpe. "Especially transition, just get into our spots of transition and just being aggressive and making the right play. We're all basketball players, we all know what to do, so just having faith in what we work on and hopefully that transitions to win."

Knowing what to do hasn't always translated on the fly.

"(It's) reading how the defense is guarding us. Sometimes they're switching and we're not having the right reads off of that," said Porter. "So we slowed down some parts of practice and walked through the different ways we can attack different coverages when teams are staying with their own vs. switching vs. top locking. All those things will help.

"I mean, we've got no choice. Any trial or tribulation can be viewed as a lesson, and that's what we're choosing to do. We're just trying to grow as a team because we're stuck with each other. And we're right where we wanna be: here. So we have no choice but to get better."

#2789. Cleveland Browns news: A big nothingburger at the trade deadline

It was a quiet day for the Cleveland Browns as the NFL trade deadline came and went on Tuesday afternoon.

Whether or not general manager Andrew Berry tried to bring anyone on board to help keep the team's playoff hopes alive is unknown.

And while there was speculation that the Minnesota Vikings, who were desperately in need of anyone with a pulse to play quarterback, might come calling about the greatest backup quarterback in NFL history, that did not come to pass as the Vikings instead decided to reunite with Kirk Cousins after three years apart.

Minnesota is returning to Cousins after cycling through eight different starting quarterbacks since the beginning of the 2024 season. If he starts this week against Green Bay, it will be the 15th time the Vikings have changed the starting quarterback from the previous week since moving on from Cousins.

Those numbers highlight something that has been evident for a long time now. While the Browns get more than their share of grief for continually messing up the quarterback position, they are far from being alone in the league in that regard.

And speaking of the Browns and quarterbacks, maybe today will be the day that head coach Todd Monken stops playing silly games and lets everyone know who the starting quarterback will be for this week's game against the Houston Texans.

#2788. 49ers put DT Gracen Halton on IR

49ers rookie defensive tackle Gracen Halton was placed on injured reserve on Tuesday.

The team’s announcement of the move did not come as any surprise. Halton broke his ankle in last Sunday’s win over the Raiders and he will miss the rest of the season as a result.

Halton joined the 49ers as a fourth-round pick in April. He started six of the team’s first eight games and ends his rookie season with 15 tackles, five tackles for loss, two sacks, a forced fumble and a fumble recovery for a touchdown.

DeForest Buckner, Osa Odighizuwa, Sebastian Valdez, and James Thompson are the remaining defensive tackles on the 49ers’ roster.

#2783. Chiefs News: Josh Downs trade is worth it in the Mahomes era

The Kansas City Chiefs have acquired wide receiver Josh Downs from the Indianapolis Colts, a move that feels inevitable in retrospect, given the constant reports of the Chiefs' interest in adding a wide receiver over the last few weeks.

In an ideal world, the Chiefs' roster is strong enough to win a Super Bowl without a midseason trade, but general manager Brett Veach rightfully acknowledged that quarterback Patrick Mahomes needed more help in order to make the most of this season.

Join fellow Chiefs fans to react to every explosive play and game-changing moment in real time.

Download the SB Nation app on iOS or Android.

Download the SB Nation app on your mobile device! For Apple devices, click here. For Android devices, click here.

Why KC Chiefs' trade for Josh Downs was worth it — especially in the Mahomes era | The Kansas City Star

Yes, we can all debate the way it came to this gap in the Chiefs' game and what they might have done otherwise along the way.

Ditto for other areas, most notably the offensive line.

And the ultimate evaluation of the merit vs. cost of this trade will hinge on how much Downs can change the dynamics of the receiving group … and where it gets the Chiefs. Maybe it won't have the spackling effect it appears to create.

But right here, right now should be the prime directive while the two-time MVP and three-time Super Bowl MVP bestrides the NFL.

2026 NFL trade deadline winners, losers: Kirk Cousins, Josh Downs, Bengals, more | ESPN

Downs doesn't just join a contender with the potential to save its offense and hold the Lombardi Trophy. He's in a contract year, and with the Chiefs' passing attack full of available targets, he could put up career numbers down the stretch. Downs can make millions in 2027 free agency should he produce as the Chiefs need him to. Had Downs become a Steeler, that path to sudden richness would have been narrower.

I personally don't think Downs has enough talent as a downfield or contested catch receiver to reach the billing the Chiefs need him to. But it's worth the swing for only an exchange of Day 3 picks.

Colts trading WR Josh Downs to Chiefs for fourth-, fifth-round picks | The Athletic

Something else: Downs has punt-return experience, which could matter for a Chiefs team that ranks 31st in expected points added per punt return, according to TruMedia.

#2782. Cardinals place QB Gardner Minshew and WR Michael Wilson on IR

The Cardinals placed quarterback Gardner Minshew and wide receiver Michael Wilson on injured reserve Tuesday, the team announced.

Minshew tore the UCL in his elbow and will miss the rest of the season. Wilson’s neck injury will end his season.

“Not the ending I had envisioned, but grateful for the journey,” Wilson wrote on social media. “Regardless of what happens, my love for the game will never change! Thank you for all the love and support. The story is still being written.”

Wilson played eight games in 2026, making 51 receptions for 546 yards and two touchdowns.

In two appearances, Minshew completed 38 of 53 passes for 354 yards and three touchdowns, and led the Cardinals to an overtime win in Dallas in Week 8. He is scheduled to be a free agent at the end of the season.

The Cardinals signed veteran quarterback Josh Johnson and rookie quarterback Joey Aguilar to back up rookie Carson Beck. Beck will get his first NFL start on Sunday against the Rams.

Aguilar was on the Jaguars’ practice squad, and Johnson was on the Bengals’ practice squad.

The Cardinals also signed receiver Phillip Dorsett II to the practice squad and released tight end Rivaldo Fairweather and running back Jermar Jefferson from the practice squad.

#2779. Ravens news: Arthur Maulet retires

Welcome back to Loose Feathers, a regular round-up of the latest news and analysis about the Baltimore Ravens. In today's edition, we cover the retirement of a recent Raven, grades for Eric DeCosta's acquisition of Jacoby Brissett, and an assessment of first-year offensive coordinator Declan Doyle, Let's dive in:

BLESSED FOR THE 9 YEARS THANK YOU EVERYONE FOR THE SUPPORT @nfl NOT TO BAD FOR A UDFA #EGBB #GODWINS

Arthur Maulet will be remembered fondly in Baltimore as a key part of Mike Macdonald's elite 2023 defense. He posted the best coverage stats of his career, and his slot blitzes were the stuff of legend. A lingering injury sidelined him for most of 2024 and led to his release the following offseason.

Maulet was originally a Saints undrafted rookie signing in 2017 and spent part of the 2018 season with the Colts. He then carved out rotational roles on defense during two-year stints with the Steelers and the Jets before arriving in Baltimore in 2023. He spent his final season in Detroit, appearing in nine games amid the Lions' many secondary injuries.

Maulet is on a fascinating list of players who played on both sides of one of the NFL's biggest rivalries. Amont them are three of his 2023 teammates – Patrick Queen, Malik Harrison, and Chuck Clark – as well as Hall of Fame safety Rod Woodson and two-time All-Pro running back Le'Von Bell. Then there are less notable but still memorable names like Mike Wallace, Miles Boykins, Chris Wormley, Diontae Johnson, DeShon Elliott, and Jacoby Jones, among others.

The October acquisition of Jurgens from the Eagles met a need. Then last week, Baltimore filled a gaping hole at quarterback by trading for quarterback Jacoby Brissett. They're hoping the 11-year veteran can keep them afloat in case Lamar Jackson returns from thumb surgery in time for a late-season playoff run.

The Ravens traded for Jacoby Brissett just to have some hope on offense. There was none with Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley on IR. It was a little surprising to see a fourth-round pick involved, though Baltimore got a fifth-round pick back. But that move without any follow-up is a bit odd: Brissett isn't going to save the season, there was nothing else added to help out but yet the Ravens traded picks to be … what exactly? The Ravens got center Cam Jurgens weeks ago, but that came at the hefty price of a second-round pick to Philadelphia.

Henley is also a team captain and the "green dot" player who calls the defense for the Chargers. At the same time, Henley is currently in the final deal of his rookie contract and no real attempts at an extension had ever come to the table. It's going to be a rough scene in that locker room as Henley has also been one of the most beloved players on the entire team during his young career.

Through the team's eight games this season, Henley has been their leading tackler with 51. He has also posted two tackles for loss, a half sack, two pass breakups, an interception, a forced fumble, and two fumble recoveries.

#2778. Malik Nabers: I felt like it was a lie for Fox broadcast to attack my character

Giants wide receiver Malik Nabers elaborated on his issues with comments made about him during last Sunday’s Fox broadcast during a press conference on Tuesday.

Fox announcers Joe Davis, Greg Olsen, and Pam Oliver discussed Nabers’s body language at several points during the game and shots of an agitated Nabers on the sideline were shown multiple times. Davis was particularly critical about Nabers’s response to a touchdown by teammate Isaiah Likely and Olsen said in response that “there’s a very fine line between being demanding, being a competitor, and all those great things” before adding that Nabers has “just got to keep it in check.”

Nabers responded to those comments and misattributed quotes that claimed Olsen called him a bad teammate in a social media post after the 19-11 win over the Eagles. Nabers said the broadcast tried to “create a narrative” that wasn’t true and shared more thoughts on the matter Tuesday.

“I just feel like the narrative on me is always all the bad body language things that people have been saying about me,” Nabers said, via a transcript from the team. “But as far as that, if it’s a problem with anybody, the cameras can stay off me. The thing about it is, don’t tell the world something that’s not me. I’m [going to] speak up for myself all the time because there’s nobody else that’s going to be able to speak up for me. So, I’m going to use my own voice and be able to speak up about me being a bad teammate. And I felt like that was just a lie, just attacking my character as a football player and as a teammate. Like I said, I’ve never been on a team where anyone has called me a bad teammate that I know of.”

Olsen took issue with the incorrect quotes about Nabers being a bad teammate and the wideout was asked if he’s heard from directly from him since Sunday. Nabers said he hasn’t, but “he can reach out and we can have a simple conversation.” Beyond that, Nabers said he’s done talking about the subject and that he’s moved on to Thursday’s game against the Commanders.
